        Idea:
Refactor pinned shot groups in the video editor to be first-class timeline entities, eliminating an entire class of "shot group disconnects" bugs that come from fragile parallel cohesion logic spread across drag, resize, sync, and render code paths.

## Why

The video editor's pinned shot groups (the colored bordered boxes that visually group N timeline clips under a single shot label) are currently a "soft" abstraction layered on top of flat `row.actions` arrays. The shot group is just a `{ shotId, trackId, clipIds: string[] }` record stored in `data.config.pinnedShotGroups`. Cohesion (the property that all the group's clips stay contiguous on the timeline) is enforced by SEPARATE bespoke logic in every code path that mutates clip positions:

- Drag: `useClipDrag` does "deferred expansion" — when the user grabs one clip in a group, it pulls the group's other clips into the drag set. Then `multi-drag-utils.applyMultiDragMoves` calls `resolveGroupOverlaps` which treats moved clips as one shifted group.
- Resize: `TimelineCanvas` resize handlers compute a `getShotGroupAdjacent` neighbor and write a paired `ResizeOverride` so the immediate neighbor follows the dragged edge.
- Sync: `usePinnedGroupSync` runs on a debounce after the underlying Shot's `images[]` changes; it rebuilds the group's clips one-by-one and shifts subsequent clips on the row.
- Track move / cross-track drag: updates `pinnedShotGroups[].trackId` via `updatePinnedShotGroupTrackIds`.
- Delete / unpin / "deconstruct" / "delete shot": each touches `pinnedShotGroups` differently.

There's no shared "the group is the unit of editing" primitive. Each path reinvents cohesion. Bugs keep emerging in different paths with the same user-visible symptom: "the shot disconnects."

On top of that, the visual contiguity check in `TimelineCanvas.tsx` (`positionedShotGroups`, around lines 446-499) iterates `group.clipIds` IN ARRAY ORDER and checks `current.start < previous.start || current.start - previous.end > snapThreshold`. **This assumes `group.clipIds` is in left-to-right spatial order, but nothing actively maintains that invariant.** `pinGroup` stores `[...selectedClipIds]` (Set iteration order, i.e. click order), and `usePinnedGroupSync` rebuilds in `desiredImages` order from the database. Once array order disagrees with spatial order by even one position, the group's border vanishes — user reports "disconnected."

We just shipped two patches for this in the drag and resize paths. The patches work for their cases but they don't address the root cause: the shot group is not a real entity in the editing model.

## Goal

Make a pinned shot group a FIRST-CLASS timeline entity. Conceptually:

- The shot group has its own bounds (`start`, `end`) on the row.
- It contains child clips with their own positions inside that bound (or relative offsets).
- Drag, resize, selection, and serialization all see the group as ONE thing by default.
- The contiguity invariant is enforced BY CONSTRUCTION, not by validation. Children of a group can never become non-contiguous because they live inside a single owning entity that lays them out.
- Operations on individual clips inside a group are explicit, intentional, and rare (the per-clip context menu is already locked down to asset-state actions only when a clip is in a pinned group, per a recent change).

## Hard requirements

1. **Eliminate the "shot disconnects" bug class.** After this refactor, no drag, resize, sync, or other normal interaction can make a pinned shot group's children become non-contiguous. The only way to break a group is the explicit "Deconstruct shot" or "Delete shot" actions on the shot label menu.

2. **No regression in user-facing behavior** for the supported flows:
   - Pinning a group from a selection.
   - Dragging a group on its row (snaps to bounds, snaps near siblings, can hit timeline start at t=0).
   - Dragging a group cross-track (entire group moves to the new row).
   - Resizing a group's outer left/right edge (stretches/shrinks the group, content reflows).
   - Resizing an interior boundary inside a group (between child clips) — re-evaluate whether this is even meaningful in the new model. If kept, must preserve cohesion.
   - The shot label menu actions (Deconstruct, Delete, Switch to Final Video, Switch to Images, Update to Latest Video, Generate Video, Jump to Shot).
   - Right-clicking a clip inside a pinned group still shows only asset-state actions per the recent change.
   - The `usePinnedGroupSync` debounce still keeps the group's children in sync with the underlying Shot's `images[]`.
   - Final video and image modes still work.
   - Auto-shift on resize from the recent megaplan still works (or is replaced by a better mechanism in the new model).

3. **Persistence stays compatible.** The on-disk timeline config schema currently stores `pinnedShotGroups: { shotId, trackId, clipIds, mode, imageClipSnapshot? }[]`. Decide whether the new model can keep this on-disk shape (with internal restructuring), or whether a schema migration is needed. If a migration is needed, it must be backward-compatible (old configs load correctly).

4. **Remotion preview rendering stays correct.** The Remotion compositions render flat clips. The refactor must not break what Remotion sees — i.e., individual clips inside a group must still be addressable for rendering, even if the editing model treats them as children of a group.

5. **Tests pass.** All existing tests in `src/tools/video-editor/` must continue to pass, OR be updated with clear justification when behavior intentionally changes. New tests should cover the cohesion-by-construction property.

## Design questions the plan must answer

The planning phase needs to investigate the codebase and propose answers to all of these. Do not guess — read the code:

1. **Data model.** Which of these is the right abstraction?
   a) "Group action" embedded in `row.actions`: the row's actions array becomes a heterogeneous list of `TimelineAction | GroupAction`, where `GroupAction` has its own start/end and a `children[]` of TimelineActions. Drag/resize see the GroupAction as a single thing.
   b) "Group resolver" overlay: keep `row.actions` as flat TimelineActions, but introduce a `GroupResolver` that, given a clipId or set of clipIds, returns the group bounds and member list. Drag/resize hooks consult the resolver and operate on the whole group when any member is touched.
   c) Something else.
   Trade off: (a) is conceptually cleaner but ripples through many types and Remotion assumptions; (b) is less invasive but still leaves cohesion as a soft property enforced by callers.

2. **Selection model.** When the user clicks one clip inside a group, do they select (i) the clip, (ii) the group, or (iii) is there a hover/click distinction (e.g., one click = group, double click = clip)? The current behavior is "click selects the clip"; the recent context-menu change limits what that clip can do. Pick a model that makes "you cannot accidentally break a group by editing one of its clips" obvious.

3. **Internal layout.** Inside a group, children are positioned. Are positions stored absolutely (each child has its own row-relative start/end) or relatively (each child has an offset from the group's start)? Relative positioning makes "shift the whole group by N seconds" trivial and impossible to break; absolute positioning means children can drift relative to each other (the original bug). Strongly prefer relative.

4. **Interior boundaries.** Today, a user can resize the boundary between two child clips of a group via the auto-shift mechanism we added. Decide whether interior boundary resize is still allowed in the new model. If yes, define it precisely. If no, document why not (likely it should not be — the user already said they don't really want per-clip operations inside a group).

5. **Sync interplay.** `usePinnedGroupSync` watches the Shot's `images[]` and rewrites the group's clips. In the new model, this becomes "rewrite the children of the group in place." Specify how this interacts with in-progress drags/resizes (it currently debounces; does that still work?).

6. **Migration / persistence.** Define the on-disk shape and the load-time conversion if needed. Old configs must load.

7. **Snap & timeline-start clamp.** Today the bug-fix from a few minutes ago lets `findBestGroupStart` clamp the group to t≥0 as a unit. In the new model, the group's `start` would be clamped directly. Make sure that still happens in every drag/resize path.

8. **Reuse of existing infrastructure.** `multi-drag-utils.ts`, `resolve-overlaps.ts`, `useClipDrag.ts`, `useClipResize.ts`, `usePinnedShotGroups.ts`, and `useShotGroups.ts` all encode pieces of cohesion. Decide which become obsolete and which get repurposed. Avoid leaving dead code.

        Plan:

# Implementation Plan: Pinned Shot Groups as First-Class Timeline Entities

## Overview

Today, a `PinnedShotGroup` is a sidecar record (`{ shotId, trackId, clipIds[], mode, imageClipSnapshot? }`) stored in `TimelineConfig.pinnedShotGroups`. Cohesion — "all clips in the group stay contiguous on the row" — is a soft invariant enforced by bespoke logic duplicated in every mutation path: `useClipDrag` (deferred expansion), `multi-drag-utils` + `resolve-overlaps` (`resolveGroupOverlaps`/`findBestGroupStart`), `useClipResize` (auto-shift), `TimelineCanvas` resize handlers (`getShotGroupAdjacent`), `usePinnedGroupSync` (debounced rebuild). On top of that, `TimelineCanvas.positionedShotGroups` (src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx:446–499) walks `group.clipIds` in **array order** and assumes that order matches spatial order — but neither `pinGroup` (Set iteration order) nor `usePinnedGroupSync` (desiredImages order) actively maintain that. Any disagreement → border vanishes → "shot disconnected."

The fix: make the group the unit of editing with **cohesion by construction via relative positioning**. A pinned group owns its `start`, `trackId`, and a spatially-ordered `children: { clipId, offset, duration }[]`. The clip entries in `TimelineConfig.clips[]` remain the Remotion source of truth, but **when a clip is a member of a pinned group, its `start` is projected from `group.start + child.offset` on every read and every write**. No code path can give a group member an independent `start`. Drag/resize operate on the group entity.

Approach: **hybrid of brief options (a) and (b)**. The canonical authoring model is group-centric (option a's mental model, with relative offsets per requirement #3). The on-the-wire shape keeps flat `clips[]` + `pinnedShotGroups[]` (option b's compatibility), but `pinnedShotGroups` gains `start` + child offsets and becomes the **sole writer** for member clip positions via a single `projectPinnedShotGroups(config)` normalizer. Remotion sees flat clips as before; the editing model sees groups.

Scope: video-editor tool only. No Supabase, no new libs. Migration is load-time and backward-compatible.

---

## Phase 1: Foundation — Model, Projection, Migration

### Step 1: Extend the PinnedShotGroup type with start + relative children (`src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Edit** `PinnedShotGroup` (src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts:169) to add:
   ```ts
   start: number;                 // row-relative seconds, single source of truth
   children: { clipId: string; offset: number; duration: number }[];
   // clipIds kept (derived, cached, spatially ordered) for backward-compat readers
   ```
   Keep `clipIds`, `mode`, `videoAssetKey`, `imageClipSnapshot` unchanged. Mark `clipIds` in a JSDoc as "derived from `children`, do not mutate directly."
2. **Invariant (documented in comment):** `children` is ordered left-to-right; `clipIds = children.map(c => c.clipId)`; for any `i`, `children[i].offset + children[i].duration <= children[i+1].offset` (contiguous-or-butted, no overlap). `start >= 0` is enforced at project time.

### Step 2: Add a single projection normalizer (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts`, new `pinned-group-projection.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-projection.ts` exporting:
   - `projectPinnedShotGroups(config: TimelineConfig): TimelineConfig` — pure function. For each group, rewrite every member clip's `{ start, end: start + duration, track: group.trackId }` using `group.start + child.offset`. Returns a new config; does not mutate.
   - `buildGroupFromClips(shotId, trackId, clipIds, config, mode, extras)` — constructs a normalized group from a selection: sorts member clips by current `start`, derives `group.start = min(start)`, computes `children[]` with `offset = clip.start - group.start`, clamps `start >= 0`.
   - `moveGroup(config, groupKey, nextStart, nextTrackId?)` — clamps `nextStart >= 0`, updates `group.start` (and `trackId`), returns new config + re-projects.
   - `resizeGroupEdge(config, groupKey, edge: 'left'|'right', deltaSeconds)` — for left edge: shifts `group.start += delta` and subtracts `delta` from the first child's offset space via rescaling child durations/offsets per policy (see Step 3). For right edge: scales or extends last child duration per policy.
   - `replaceGroupChildren(config, groupKey, nextChildren)` — used by sync; preserves `group.start`, writes new children in order, re-projects.
   - `normalizeChildren(children)` — forces contiguous ordering (sort by offset, collapse micro-gaps inside group, reassign offsets). Called from every writer above.
2. **Call** `projectPinnedShotGroups` at the start of `assembleTimelineData` (src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts:332) so every downstream `TimelineData` reader sees projected clip positions. Also call it inside `rowsToConfig` (src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts:201) as the final step before returning, guaranteeing no write can produce an un-projected config.

### Step 3: Decide the resize policy for group edges (part of Step 2 implementation)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Policy (to confirm with user — see Questions):** Left/right edge resize **uniformly scales child durations** so the group rescales as a unit. Interior-boundary resize is **removed** (see Question 4 + Assumption). `useClipResize` will short-circuit for clips inside a pinned group and delegate to `resizeGroupEdge` only when the dragged edge is the group's outer left or right edge; interior handles are disabled on group members in `ClipAction.tsx`.
2. Implement the uniform-scale math inside `resizeGroupEdge` and document it in the JSDoc.

### Step 4: Load-time migration for legacy configs (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/serialize.ts` or `timeline-data.ts:157`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Edit** `configToRows` / `buildTimelineData` entry (src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts:157) so that after loading, any `PinnedShotGroup` missing `start`/`children` is upgraded: look up each `clipId` in `config.clips[]`, sort by current `start`, derive `start = min(start)`, compute `children` offsets. If a referenced clipId is missing, drop it (existing behavior in `useShotGroups`). This runs before projection.
2. **Keep** on-disk shape additive (`start`, `children` are new optional fields). Old configs load → migrate in-memory → re-serialize with new fields on next save. **No schema version bump needed.**
3. **Update** `ALLOWED_TOP_LEVEL_KEYS` path allowlists in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/serialize.ts:98+` if the validator rejects unknown keys on the group object (verify — likely unchanged since validation is shallow).

### Step 5: Unit tests for the projection + migration layer (new `pinned-group-projection.test.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-projection.test.ts`:
   - `projectPinnedShotGroups` rewrites member clip starts from `group.start + offset`.
   - Migration: legacy group (no `start`/`children`) is upgraded correctly from clip positions.
   - `moveGroup` clamps negative `start` to 0.
   - `resizeGroupEdge('left', delta)` and `('right', delta)` rescale children uniformly and preserve contiguity.
   - `replaceGroupChildren` preserves `group.start` and normalizes ordering.
   - **Cohesion-by-construction property test**: after any sequence of `moveGroup` / `resizeGroupEdge` / `replaceGroupChildren` followed by `projectPinnedShotGroups`, for every group `children[i].offset + children[i].duration === children[i+1].offset` and `group.clipIds` is spatially sorted.

---

## Phase 2: Write-Path Refactor — Drag, Resize, Sync use Group Entity

### Step 6: Rewrite drag cohesion to operate on the group entity (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/multi-drag-utils.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/resolve-overlaps.ts`)
**Scope:** Large

1. **Edit** `useClipDrag.ts` (src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.ts:75, 215–237, 278–281): on pointerdown over a clip in a pinned group, **do not** record per-clip offsets for the group's members. Instead, record a single `GroupDragEntry { groupKey, originStart, originTrackId }`. Remove the deferred-expansion code path entirely and replace it with direct group-entity tracking. Non-group clips keep the existing per-clip drag path.
2. **Edit** `planMultiDragMoves` / `applyMultiDragMoves` (src/tools/video-editor/lib/multi-drag-utils.ts:248, 302) to accept a heterogeneous move list of `{ kind: 'clip', ... } | { kind: 'group', groupKey, nextStart, nextTrackId }`. Group moves call `moveGroup(config, ...)` and skip per-clip overlap resolution for members.
3. **Simplify** `resolveGroupOverlaps` / `findBestGroupStart` (src/tools/video-editor/lib/resolve-overlaps.ts:81, 216): they continue to exist but now operate on a single `{ start, end }` bounding box passed in by the caller (derived from `group.start` + last child's `offset + duration`). Delete the collective-start-from-individual-clips logic (src/tools/video-editor/lib/resolve-overlaps.ts:255–257) — it's no longer needed because the caller hands in the true group extent.
4. **Edit** `updatePinnedShotGroupTrackIdsFromClipTrackMap` (src/tools/video-editor/lib/multi-drag-utils.ts:47–80): keep the function but simplify — group moves already carry `nextTrackId`, so this becomes a fallback for non-group-aware drag paths and can be deleted once all callers migrate (check cross-references before removing).
5. **Update** `findBestGroupStart` clamp (t ≥ 0) to remain the single authority for "group start must be ≥ 0" — called from `moveGroup`. The per-clip clamp in the drag path is removed for group members.

### Step 7: Rewrite resize cohesion to operate on the group edge (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResize.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx`)
**Scope:** Large

1. **Edit** `useClipResize.ts` (src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResize.ts:1–150): detect if the resized clip is a member of a pinned group (via `config.pinnedShotGroups`). If so:
   - If the dragged edge corresponds to the group's outer left edge (first child's left) or outer right edge (last child's right), call `resizeGroupEdge` and commit via `applyEdit({ type: 'pinnedShotGroups', ... })`.
   - Otherwise (interior handle), **reject the drag** — do not start a resize session. Complements Step 11's UI change disabling interior handles.
2. **Edit** `TimelineCanvas.tsx` resize handlers and `getShotGroupAdjacent` (find via grep; called near src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx resize handler region): remove the bespoke paired-ResizeOverride logic. The group-edge resize now reflows children automatically via `resizeGroupEdge`; no neighbor-write is needed.
3. **Edit** `positionedShotGroups` (src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx:446–499): replace the in-array-order contiguity check with a direct read of `group.start` and the last child's `offset + duration` from the (now-projected) config. The contiguity check can be **deleted** — it's tautologically true after projection. Render the bounding box directly from group geometry.

### Step 8: Update pinned-group sync to use replaceGroupChildren (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Edit** `pinGroup` (src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts:98–112): call `buildGroupFromClips` so the created group has `start` + `children` in spatial order from day one, not `[...selectedClipIds]` in Set order.
2. **Edit** `updatePinnedGroup` (src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts:121–144): normalize via the new helpers; never write a raw `clipIds` array.
3. **Edit** `usePinnedGroupSync` (src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts:153–404): when `desiredImages` changes, call `replaceGroupChildren(config, groupKey, nextChildren)`. `nextChildren` is computed by walking `desiredImages` in order and looking up each image's clip's current `duration` (or default). `group.start` is preserved. The 300ms debounce is unchanged; interaction with in-progress drags is handled by the existing "is any drag/resize active" guard (verify at sync call site — if not present, add one: skip sync while `useDragCoordinator` reports active).
4. **Edit** `reconcilePinnedShotGroupsInRows` (src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineCommit.ts:108–150+): operate on `children[]` rather than `clipIds[]`. Filter dead children; preserve `start`. For `video` mode reconstruction from `imageClipSnapshot`, build `children` from the snapshot the same way.

### Step 9: Update deconstruct / delete / unpin / track-move paths (`src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ShotGroupContextMenu.tsx` + call sites)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Edit** Deconstruct / Delete / Unpin handlers (locate via grep from `ShotGroupContextMenu.tsx`) to just remove the group from `pinnedShotGroups`. After removal, the member clips retain their last projected `start`s (which is the desired behavior — they stay where they were visually, now as free clips).
2. **Edit** Switch-to-Final-Video / Switch-to-Images (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useSwitchToFinalVideo.ts` and siblings): these currently rewrite `clipIds` + `imageClipSnapshot`. Update to rewrite `children` instead, preserving `group.start`.

## Phase 3: Read-Path Cleanup + UI Guards

### Step 10: Selection model — single click still selects the inner clip (no UX change)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Decision (Question 2):** Keep current behavior: clicking a clip inside a group selects the clip (context-menu is already locked to asset actions per the recent change). Do **not** introduce click-vs-double-click disambiguation. The "can't break the group" property comes from cohesion-by-construction in the write path, not from selection gating.
2. **Verify** `useTimelineSelection` / `useMultiSelect` do not hold per-clip positions that could become stale after group moves (they shouldn't — selection is ID-based).

### Step 11: Disable interior resize handles on group members (`src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Edit** `ClipAction.tsx` (src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.tsx:23, 162–240): when `isInPinnedShotGroup`, render outer-edge-only handles IF the clip is the first or last child of the group; render no handles otherwise. (Or: render no per-clip handles at all for group members, and render two group-level handles as part of the group overlay in `TimelineCanvas.tsx`. Pick the second option — simpler wiring, keeps handle state on the group, matches the "group is the unit of editing" intent.)
2. **Edit** `TimelineCanvas.tsx` `positionedShotGroups` render path to emit left/right resize handles anchored to the group's bounding box. Wire them into `useClipResize` via a new `startGroupEdgeResize(groupKey, edge)` entrypoint.

### Step 12: Delete now-dead cohesion code
**Scope:** Small

1. **Delete** or inline: `getShotGroupAdjacent` (if no callers remain), the array-order contiguity check in `positionedShotGroups`, deferred-expansion fields in `useClipDrag` state, `resolveGroupOverlaps`'s collective-extent calculation, auto-shift transaction logic from `useClipResize` that only existed to stitch interior resize. Check for callers before deleting. Per CLAUDE.md "every change should make the codebase smaller or more explicit."
2. **Audit** `useShotGroups.ts` (src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useShotGroups.ts:26–55) — the "filter out groups with no live clipIds" can now trust `children` as authoritative. Simplify.

## Phase 4: Validation

### Step 13: Update + add tests (`src/tools/video-editor/**/*.test.*`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Update** existing tests that will break because the data shape changed:

2. **Add** a new cohesion-by-construction test file `src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-cohesion.test.ts`:
   - Randomized sequence of `moveGroup` / `resizeGroupEdge` / `replaceGroupChildren` operations. After each, assert the contiguity + spatial-ordering invariant holds via `projectPinnedShotGroups`.
   - Cross-track drag: group moves entirely to the new `trackId`; all member clips' `track` field updates.
   - Clamp test: `moveGroup` to negative start is clamped to 0 and no child goes negative.
3. **Run** focused targeted tests first, then the full `src/tools/video-editor/` suite, then `npx tsc --noEmit` for the whole repo.

### Step 14: Manual smoke test per Hard Requirement #2 (info)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Smoke-test flows listed in the brief**: pin a group, drag on row (incl. to t=0), drag cross-track, resize outer edges, attempt interior resize (should be blocked), verify shot label menu actions, verify asset-state context menu on inner clips, verify sync after Shot.images changes, verify final-video/image mode switching, verify Remotion preview renders identically. (Listed here for the reviewer; not machine-checked.)

---

## Execution Order
1. Phase 1 (model + projection + migration + unit tests) — cheap, isolated, green-lit by unit tests before any UI touches.
2. Phase 2 Step 8 (sync) in parallel-safe order after Step 6/7? No — do Step 6 (drag) first, Step 7 (resize) second, then Step 8 (sync) — because sync's `replaceGroupChildren` depends on the normalizer from Phase 1 being solid and the drag/resize paths no longer racing sync.
3. Phase 3 (UI guards + dead-code deletion) after Phase 2 compiles.
4. Phase 4 (tests) runs continuously; broader suite + tsc last.

## Validation Order
1. `pinned-group-projection.test.ts` (Step 5) — lightest, validates the core invariant.
2. `pinned-group-cohesion.test.ts` (Step 13) — property test for the whole write path.
3. Updated existing unit tests in `src/tools/video-editor/`.
4. `npx tsc --noEmit`.
5. Full video-editor test run.
6. Manual smoke (Step 14, info-level).

        - These are known accepted limitations. Do not re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse, broadens them, or fails to contain them.
        - Prefix every new concern with a subsystem tag followed by a colon, for example `Timeout recovery: retry backoff remains brittle`.

For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

        Idea:
Refactor pinned shot groups in the video editor to be first-class timeline entities, eliminating an entire class of "shot group disconnects" bugs that come from fragile parallel cohesion logic spread across drag, resize, sync, and render code paths.

## Why

The video editor's pinned shot groups (the colored bordered boxes that visually group N timeline clips under a single shot label) are currently a "soft" abstraction layered on top of flat `row.actions` arrays. The shot group is just a `{ shotId, trackId, clipIds: string[] }` record stored in `data.config.pinnedShotGroups`. Cohesion (the property that all the group's clips stay contiguous on the timeline) is enforced by SEPARATE bespoke logic in every code path that mutates clip positions:

- Drag: `useClipDrag` does "deferred expansion" — when the user grabs one clip in a group, it pulls the group's other clips into the drag set. Then `multi-drag-utils.applyMultiDragMoves` calls `resolveGroupOverlaps` which treats moved clips as one shifted group.
- Resize: `TimelineCanvas` resize handlers compute a `getShotGroupAdjacent` neighbor and write a paired `ResizeOverride` so the immediate neighbor follows the dragged edge.
- Sync: `usePinnedGroupSync` runs on a debounce after the underlying Shot's `images[]` changes; it rebuilds the group's clips one-by-one and shifts subsequent clips on the row.
- Track move / cross-track drag: updates `pinnedShotGroups[].trackId` via `updatePinnedShotGroupTrackIds`.
- Delete / unpin / "deconstruct" / "delete shot": each touches `pinnedShotGroups` differently.

There's no shared "the group is the unit of editing" primitive. Each path reinvents cohesion. Bugs keep emerging in different paths with the same user-visible symptom: "the shot disconnects."

On top of that, the visual contiguity check in `TimelineCanvas.tsx` (`positionedShotGroups`, around lines 446-499) iterates `group.clipIds` IN ARRAY ORDER and checks `current.start < previous.start || current.start - previous.end > snapThreshold`. **This assumes `group.clipIds` is in left-to-right spatial order, but nothing actively maintains that invariant.** `pinGroup` stores `[...selectedClipIds]` (Set iteration order, i.e. click order), and `usePinnedGroupSync` rebuilds in `desiredImages` order from the database. Once array order disagrees with spatial order by even one position, the group's border vanishes — user reports "disconnected."

We just shipped two patches for this in the drag and resize paths. The patches work for their cases but they don't address the root cause: the shot group is not a real entity in the editing model.

## Goal

Make a pinned shot group a FIRST-CLASS timeline entity. Conceptually:

- The shot group has its own bounds (`start`, `end`) on the row.
- It contains child clips with their own positions inside that bound (or relative offsets).
- Drag, resize, selection, and serialization all see the group as ONE thing by default.
- The contiguity invariant is enforced BY CONSTRUCTION, not by validation. Children of a group can never become non-contiguous because they live inside a single owning entity that lays them out.
- Operations on individual clips inside a group are explicit, intentional, and rare (the per-clip context menu is already locked down to asset-state actions only when a clip is in a pinned group, per a recent change).

## Hard requirements

1. **Eliminate the "shot disconnects" bug class.** After this refactor, no drag, resize, sync, or other normal interaction can make a pinned shot group's children become non-contiguous. The only way to break a group is the explicit "Deconstruct shot" or "Delete shot" actions on the shot label menu.

2. **No regression in user-facing behavior** for the supported flows:
   - Pinning a group from a selection.
   - Dragging a group on its row (snaps to bounds, snaps near siblings, can hit timeline start at t=0).
   - Dragging a group cross-track (entire group moves to the new row).
   - Resizing a group's outer left/right edge (stretches/shrinks the group, content reflows).
   - Resizing an interior boundary inside a group (between child clips) — re-evaluate whether this is even meaningful in the new model. If kept, must preserve cohesion.
   - The shot label menu actions (Deconstruct, Delete, Switch to Final Video, Switch to Images, Update to Latest Video, Generate Video, Jump to Shot).
   - Right-clicking a clip inside a pinned group still shows only asset-state actions per the recent change.
   - The `usePinnedGroupSync` debounce still keeps the group's children in sync with the underlying Shot's `images[]`.
   - Final video and image modes still work.
   - Auto-shift on resize from the recent megaplan still works (or is replaced by a better mechanism in the new model).

3. **Persistence stays compatible.** The on-disk timeline config schema currently stores `pinnedShotGroups: { shotId, trackId, clipIds, mode, imageClipSnapshot? }[]`. Decide whether the new model can keep this on-disk shape (with internal restructuring), or whether a schema migration is needed. If a migration is needed, it must be backward-compatible (old configs load correctly).

4. **Remotion preview rendering stays correct.** The Remotion compositions render flat clips. The refactor must not break what Remotion sees — i.e., individual clips inside a group must still be addressable for rendering, even if the editing model treats them as children of a group.

5. **Tests pass.** All existing tests in `src/tools/video-editor/` must continue to pass, OR be updated with clear justification when behavior intentionally changes. New tests should cover the cohesion-by-construction property.

## Design questions the plan must answer

The planning phase needs to investigate the codebase and propose answers to all of these. Do not guess — read the code:

1. **Data model.** Which of these is the right abstraction?
   a) "Group action" embedded in `row.actions`: the row's actions array becomes a heterogeneous list of `TimelineAction | GroupAction`, where `GroupAction` has its own start/end and a `children[]` of TimelineActions. Drag/resize see the GroupAction as a single thing.
   b) "Group resolver" overlay: keep `row.actions` as flat TimelineActions, but introduce a `GroupResolver` that, given a clipId or set of clipIds, returns the group bounds and member list. Drag/resize hooks consult the resolver and operate on the whole group when any member is touched.
   c) Something else.
   Trade off: (a) is conceptually cleaner but ripples through many types and Remotion assumptions; (b) is less invasive but still leaves cohesion as a soft property enforced by callers.

2. **Selection model.** When the user clicks one clip inside a group, do they select (i) the clip, (ii) the group, or (iii) is there a hover/click distinction (e.g., one click = group, double click = clip)? The current behavior is "click selects the clip"; the recent context-menu change limits what that clip can do. Pick a model that makes "you cannot accidentally break a group by editing one of its clips" obvious.

3. **Internal layout.** Inside a group, children are positioned. Are positions stored absolutely (each child has its own row-relative start/end) or relatively (each child has an offset from the group's start)? Relative positioning makes "shift the whole group by N seconds" trivial and impossible to break; absolute positioning means children can drift relative to each other (the original bug). Strongly prefer relative.

4. **Interior boundaries.** Today, a user can resize the boundary between two child clips of a group via the auto-shift mechanism we added. Decide whether interior boundary resize is still allowed in the new model. If yes, define it precisely. If no, document why not (likely it should not be — the user already said they don't really want per-clip operations inside a group).

5. **Sync interplay.** `usePinnedGroupSync` watches the Shot's `images[]` and rewrites the group's clips. In the new model, this becomes "rewrite the children of the group in place." Specify how this interacts with in-progress drags/resizes (it currently debounces; does that still work?).

6. **Migration / persistence.** Define the on-disk shape and the load-time conversion if needed. Old configs must load.

7. **Snap & timeline-start clamp.** Today the bug-fix from a few minutes ago lets `findBestGroupStart` clamp the group to t≥0 as a unit. In the new model, the group's `start` would be clamped directly. Make sure that still happens in every drag/resize path.

8. **Reuse of existing infrastructure.** `multi-drag-utils.ts`, `resolve-overlaps.ts`, `useClipDrag.ts`, `useClipResize.ts`, `usePinnedShotGroups.ts`, and `useShotGroups.ts` all encode pieces of cohesion. Decide which become obsolete and which get repurposed. Avoid leaving dead code.

## Files most likely to change

Start by reading these to ground the plan. Many more files will surface — explore from these as anchors:

- Data shape and config:
  - src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts (look for PinnedShotGroup, TimelineConfig)
  - src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts (resolve config -> TimelineData)
- Group accessors and sync:
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useShotGroups.ts
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts (pinGroup, unpinGroup, updatePinnedGroup, usePinnedGroupSync)
- Drag:
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.ts (expandPinnedGroupDrag, deferredPinnedGroupClipIds)
  - src/tools/video-editor/lib/multi-drag-utils.ts (planMultiDragMoves, applyMultiDragMoves, updatePinnedShotGroupTrackIds)
  - src/tools/video-editor/lib/resolve-overlaps.ts (resolveGroupOverlaps, findBestGroupStart)
  - src/tools/video-editor/lib/snap-edges.ts
- Resize:
  - src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx (resize handlers, getShotGroupAdjacent, positionedShotGroups)
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResize.ts (auto-shift transaction logic from the recent megaplan)
- Render and editor wiring:
  - src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx
  - src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.tsx
  - src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ShotGroupContextMenu.tsx
- Provider / state:
  - src/tools/video-editor/contexts/VideoEditorProvider.tsx
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineState.types.ts
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineState.contexts.ts
- Final video / image mode switching:
  - src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useSwitchToFinalVideo.ts (or similarly named)
- Remotion / preview:
  - Whatever in src/tools/video-editor/compositions/ and src/tools/video-editor/components/PreviewPanel/ reads the timeline config -- verify it does not depend on flat row.actions in a way the new model breaks.

## Constraints

- Scope is the video-editor tool only. No DB schema changes, no edge functions, no migrations to Supabase. (The on-disk config is a JSON blob in the user's project file.)
- Follow the rules in CLAUDE.md at the repo root and in /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/CLAUDE.md.
- Prefer Edit over Write. No new docs unless explicitly necessary.
- Don't introduce new state management libraries or routing.
- Don't bundle unrelated cleanups. Keep the diff focused on the shot-group refactor.
- Pre-existing failing tests (one in TimelineCanvas.test.tsx about data-shot-group-kind="pinned") are unrelated and should be left alone.
- Reuse existing UI primitives in src/shared/components/ui/. No new color tokens.

## What success looks like

1. After the refactor, manually attempting to break a pinned shot group via any combination of normal drag, resize, snap, multi-select, or cross-track drag is impossible. The only way to break a group is the explicit Deconstruct/Delete actions.
2. Type check passes (npx tsc --noEmit).
3. Existing video-editor tests pass, plus new tests covering the cohesion-by-construction property.
4. The Remotion preview still renders the same output for an unchanged timeline.
5. The diff is concentrated in the video-editor tool with a clear before/after for the new abstraction.

        Plan:

# Implementation Plan: Pinned Shot Groups as First-Class Timeline Entities (rev 2)

## Overview

Pinned shot groups today are a sidecar `{ shotId, trackId, clipIds[], mode, imageClipSnapshot? }` record on `TimelineConfig`, with cohesion enforced by bespoke per-path logic (drag deferred-expansion, resize paired-overrides, sync rebuild, contiguity check assuming `clipIds` is in spatial order). The disconnect bug class comes from those code paths drifting out of sync with one another and from `clipIds` not actually being maintained in spatial order.

**Approach (settled):** Hybrid — group-centric authoring model with relative child offsets (`start: number`, `children: { clipId, offset, duration }[]`), flat `TimelineConfig.clips[]` retained as the Remotion source of truth, projected from `group.start + child.offset` via a single `projectPinnedShotGroups(config)` normalizer. Cohesion-by-construction: there is no API to position a group member independently.

**What revision 2 fixes (from gate flags):**
1. Projection wiring is moved upstream into `buildDataFromCurrentRegistry` so `resolvedConfig`, `signature`, and preview state all see projected positions. `assembleTimelineData` itself does NOT mutate (FLAG-001, correctness-1).
2. Every clone helper (`migrate.ts:clonePinnedShotGroups`, `timeline-data.ts:91-100`, `usePinnedShotGroups.ts:31-39`) is updated to deep-copy `start` + `children` (FLAG-004, all_locations-1).
3. `useExternalDrop` shot-drop creation path goes through `buildGroupFromClips` (FLAG-003, scope-3, issue_hints-3).
4. Delete shot semantics preserved exactly: still deletes member clips and then unpins (FLAG-005, issue_hints-1).
5. Broader edit surface — `useClipEditing` (split/delete), `useKeyboardShortcuts` (delete/split/ArrowUp/ArrowDown), `useTimelineTrackManagement.moveSelectedClipsToTrack` — gets a single shared "promote selected clip(s) to enclosing pinned group" guard so any clip-level mutation that targets a group member is rerouted to the group (FLAG-002, issue_hints-2, scope-1, scope-2, callers-1, all_locations-3).
6. `useClipEditing` config-edit path that calls `applyEdit({ type: 'config', ... })` is also wrapped in projection at the commit boundary (callers-2).
7. Sync vs. in-progress drag guard is **explicitly added** to `useDragCoordinator`, not "verified" (correctness-2).
8. Validation surface adds `useExternalDrop.test.tsx` and `useTimelineTrackManagement.test.ts` updates (all_locations-2).

Scope: video-editor tool only. No Supabase. Migration is additive and load-time.

---

## Phase 1: Foundation — Model, Projection, Clone Helpers, Migration

### Step 1: Extend the PinnedShotGroup type (`src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Edit** `PinnedShotGroup` (`src/tools/video-editor/types/index.ts:169`) to add:
   ```ts
   start: number;                                                  // row-relative seconds
   children: { clipId: string; offset: number; duration: number }[]; // spatial order, relative to start
   ```
   Keep `clipIds`, `mode`, `videoAssetKey`, `imageClipSnapshot`. JSDoc on `clipIds`: "derived from `children`; do not mutate directly."
2. **Document the invariant** in a comment: `children` is spatially ordered; `clipIds === children.map(c => c.clipId)`; for any `i`, `children[i].offset + children[i].duration <= children[i+1].offset`; `start >= 0`.

### Step 2: Add projection + group operations module (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-projection.ts` — new)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`. Pure functions, no React imports:
   - `projectPinnedShotGroups(config: TimelineConfig): TimelineConfig` — returns a NEW config object. For each group, rewrites every member clip in `config.clips[]` with `start = group.start + child.offset`, `end = start + child.duration`, `track = group.trackId`. Non-member clips untouched.
   - `buildGroupFromClips(args: { shotId, trackId, clipIds, config, mode, extras? })` — sorts clipIds by current `clip.start`, computes `group.start = max(0, min(clip.start))`, builds `children` with offsets relative to that start. Used by `pinGroup` AND `useExternalDrop`.
   - `moveGroup(config, groupKey, nextStart, nextTrackId?)` — clamps `nextStart >= 0`, updates `group.start` (and optional `trackId`), returns config (caller may then re-project).
   - `resizeGroupEdge(config, groupKey, edge: 'left'|'right', deltaSeconds)` — uniform-scale policy: scales all child durations by the new total / old total; recomputes offsets contiguously. Left edge also shifts `group.start`.
   - `replaceGroupChildren(config, groupKey, nextChildren)` — preserves `group.start`; writes new children in spatial order (caller is responsible for ordering).
   - `normalizeChildren(children)` — sort by offset, butt-collapse internal gaps, renumber offsets so `children[i+1].offset = children[i].offset + children[i].duration`.
   - `findEnclosingPinnedGroup(config, clipId): PinnedShotGroup | null` — used by the broader edit surface in Phase 2.
2. **Critical:** every group writer above ends by calling `normalizeChildren` then returning a new config. Callers run `projectPinnedShotGroups` once at the commit boundary (Step 3).

### Step 3: Wire projection into `buildDataFromCurrentRegistry` AND `applyEdit` commit boundary (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-save-utils.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineCommit.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Edit** `buildDataFromCurrentRegistry` (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-save-utils.ts:23–51`): immediately after `repairConfig(migrateToFlatTracks(...))`, call `migratedConfig = projectPinnedShotGroups(migratedConfig)` BEFORE `resolvedConfig` is constructed. This guarantees `resolvedConfig.clips`, the downstream `signature`, and the `assembleTimelineData` output all see the same projected positions. **Do not project inside `assembleTimelineData`** — keep it as a derivation step at the front of every materialization path.
2. **Edit** `useTimelineCommit.ts` `applyEdit` for type `'config'` and type `'pinnedShotGroups'` (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineCommit.ts`): on entry, run the incoming `config`/override through `projectPinnedShotGroups` so `useClipEditing` callers that pass whole `resolvedConfig` objects (`useClipEditing.ts:337–386`, `useTimelineTrackManagement.ts:99–127`) cannot bypass projection. For type `'rows'`, projection still happens via `buildDataFromCurrentRegistry`'s downstream re-build path.
3. **Verify** there is no second materialization path that builds rows from raw `config`. Grep for callers of `assembleTimelineData` and ensure they all go through `buildDataFromCurrentRegistry` or carry an already-projected config.

### Step 4: Update every clone helper (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/migrate.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Edit** `clonePinnedShotGroups` in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/migrate.ts:163–172` to also deep-copy `start` and `children`:
   ```ts
   pinnedShotGroups?.map((group) => ({
     ...group,
     clipIds: [...group.clipIds],
     children: group.children?.map((c) => ({ ...c })),
     imageClipSnapshot: group.imageClipSnapshot?.map((s) => ({ ...s, meta: { ...s.meta } })),
   }));
   ```
2. **Edit** the equivalent clone helper in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/timeline-data.ts:91–100` the same way.
3. **Edit** the per-mutation clone in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts:31–39` the same way.
4. **Audit** with `Grep "clipIds:\s*\[\.\.\."` inside `src/tools/video-editor/` to catch any other ad-hoc clone of a `PinnedShotGroup`. Each must include `children`.

### Step 5: Load-time migration for legacy configs (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/migrate.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** an upgrader inside `repairConfig` (or a new helper called from it, in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/migrate.ts`) that, for any `PinnedShotGroup` missing `start` or `children`: looks up each `clipId` in `config.clips[]`, drops missing ids, sorts by `clip.start`, sets `group.start = max(0, min(clip.start))`, builds `children` with offsets relative to that start and `duration = clip.end - clip.start`. Idempotent — if `start`+`children` are present, no-op.
2. **Confirm** `repairConfig` is called from `buildDataFromCurrentRegistry` (it is — `timeline-save-utils.ts:28`), so legacy configs are upgraded before projection.
3. **No schema version bump.** Old configs load → upgrade in-memory → re-serialize with new fields on next save.

### Step 6: Unit tests for projection + group ops + migration (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-projection.test.ts` — new)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** `src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-projection.test.ts`:
   - `projectPinnedShotGroups` rewrites member clip starts; non-members untouched.
   - `buildGroupFromClips` derives `start` and `children` from clip positions in spatial order.
   - `moveGroup` clamps `nextStart < 0` to 0.
   - `resizeGroupEdge('left' | 'right', delta)` rescales children uniformly and preserves contiguity.
   - `replaceGroupChildren` preserves `group.start`.
   - Migration: a legacy group `{ shotId, trackId, clipIds }` with no `start`/`children` upgrades correctly when fed through `repairConfig`.
   - **Property test:** for any sequence of `moveGroup` / `resizeGroupEdge` / `replaceGroupChildren` followed by `projectPinnedShotGroups`, every group satisfies `children[i].offset + children[i].duration === children[i+1].offset` and `clipIds.map(id => clips.find(...).start)` is monotonic.

---

## Phase 2: Write-Path Refactor — Drag, Resize, Sync, **Edit Surface**, Creation

### Step 7: Drag — single GroupDragEntry (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/multi-drag-utils.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/resolve-overlaps.ts`)
**Scope:** Large

1. **Edit** `useClipDrag.ts` (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.ts:75, 215–237, 278–281`): on pointerdown over a clip in a pinned group, record `GroupDragEntry { groupKey, originStart, originTrackId }`. Delete deferred-expansion (`expandPinnedGroupDrag`, `deferredPinnedGroupClipIds`).
2. **Edit** `planMultiDragMoves` / `applyMultiDragMoves` (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/multi-drag-utils.ts:248, 302`) to accept heterogeneous moves `{ kind: 'clip', ... } | { kind: 'group', groupKey, nextStart, nextTrackId }`. Group moves call `moveGroup` then re-project.
3. **Simplify** `resolveGroupOverlaps` / `findBestGroupStart` (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/resolve-overlaps.ts:81, 216`): they now take a `{ start, end }` extent passed in by the caller (derived from `group.start` + last child's `offset + duration`). Delete the collective-extent calculation at `resolve-overlaps.ts:255–257`.
4. `findBestGroupStart` remains the t≥0 clamp authority; `moveGroup` calls it.
5. **Audit** `updatePinnedShotGroupTrackIdsFromClipTrackMap` (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/multi-drag-utils.ts:47–80`) callers; delete if all migrate to group-aware moves.

### Step 8: Resize — outer-edge only via `resizeGroupEdge` (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResize.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Edit** `useClipResize.ts`: if the resized clip is inside a pinned group (use `findEnclosingPinnedGroup`), reject the resize. Outer-edge resize is initiated from a new group-handle entrypoint, not from per-clip handles.
2. **Edit** `TimelineCanvas.tsx` resize handlers + `getShotGroupAdjacent`: delete the bespoke paired-`ResizeOverride` logic.
3. **Edit** `positionedShotGroups` (`src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx:446–499`): replace the in-array-order contiguity check with a direct read of `group.start` + `last(children).offset + duration`. The contiguity check is **deleted**.
4. **Add** group-edge resize handles to the `positionedShotGroups` overlay; wire them to a new `startGroupEdgeResize(groupKey, edge)` entrypoint in `useClipResize`.

### Step 9: Sync — `replaceGroupChildren` + explicit drag/resize guard (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useDragCoordinator.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Edit** `useDragCoordinator.ts` to expose `isInteractionActive(): boolean` (true while any drag or resize session is in progress). If a similar flag already exists, reuse it; otherwise add the minimum state needed (one ref + accessor; the coordinator already tracks active sessions).
2. **Edit** `usePinnedGroupSync` (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts:153–404`): inside the debounced effect callback, check `isInteractionActive()` first; if true, skip this tick and re-arm the debounce so sync runs after the interaction ends. Apply changes via `replaceGroupChildren(config, groupKey, nextChildren)`, where `nextChildren` is built by walking `desiredImages` in order with each image's clip `duration` (or default). `group.start` is preserved.
3. **Edit** `pinGroup` (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts:98–112`) to delegate to `buildGroupFromClips`.
4. **Edit** `updatePinnedGroup` (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usePinnedShotGroups.ts:121–144`) to never write a raw `clipIds` array — always go through the new helpers.
5. **Edit** `reconcilePinnedShotGroupsInRows` (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineCommit.ts:108–150+`) to operate on `children[]`, preserving `group.start`. Filter dead children.

### Step 10: Creation paths — `useExternalDrop` (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useExternalDrop.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Edit** `useExternalDrop.ts:128–193`: instead of constructing the new pinned group inline at line 176–181 (`{ shotId, trackId, clipIds: createdClipIds, mode: 'images' }`), call `buildGroupFromClips({ shotId: shot.id, trackId: resolvedTarget.trackId, clipIds: createdClipIds, config: workingData.config, mode: 'images' })`. This guarantees the new group has `start`/`children` from the moment it enters the system.
2. The subsequent `applyEdit({ type: 'rows', ... pinnedShotGroupsOverride })` is unchanged — projection at the commit boundary in Step 3 handles the rest.

### Step 11: Broader edit surface — promote clip-level mutations on group members to group-level operations (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipEditing.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useKeyboardShortcuts.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineTrackManagement.ts`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Add** a single shared helper in `src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-projection.ts`: `categorizeSelection(clipIds, config) → { freeClips: string[], affectedGroups: PinnedShotGroup[] }`. Given a selection, returns which selected clips are free and which groups are touched (with full membership).
2. **Edit** `useClipEditing.ts:220–236` (single-clip delete) and `:351–386` (split): if the targeted clip is a group member, **block split entirely** (it would break cohesion; same rationale as interior resize) and **redirect delete** to delete-then-unpin the whole group (mirrors the existing Delete-shot menu action — see Step 12). Document why split is blocked in a code comment.
3. **Edit** `useKeyboardShortcuts.ts:68–110`: keyboard delete/split for a primary-selected group member follows the same redirect rules. ArrowUp/ArrowDown track move on a group member is rerouted through the group-move primitive (calls `moveGroup` with `nextTrackId`).
4. **Edit** `useTimelineTrackManagement.ts:244–331` `moveSelectedClipsToTrack`: before grouping selection by row, run `categorizeSelection`. For each affected group, plan a single group track-move via `moveGroup`. Free clips keep the existing per-clip path.
5. **Edit** `useTimelineState.contexts.ts:98–161` only if a context value's signature must change to expose new entrypoints (e.g., `startGroupEdgeResize`). Otherwise leave it alone.

### Step 12: Shot-group context-menu actions — preserve current semantics (`src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ShotGroupContextMenu.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Verify and preserve** the existing wiring at `src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineEditor.tsx:691–697`:
   - **Unpin / Deconstruct** = `unpinGroup(shotId, trackId)` only. Member clips remain in place at their last projected positions.
   - **Delete shot** = `handleDeleteClips(group.clipIds)` THEN `unpinGroup(...)`. Both clips and the group entry are removed. **Do not change this behavior** — rev 1 misread it.
2. **Edit** `useSwitchToFinalVideo` and the corresponding switch-to-images handler so that when they rewrite group state, they go through `replaceGroupChildren` (preserving `group.start`) rather than rewriting `clipIds` directly. The existing `imageClipSnapshot` payload is unchanged.

---

## Phase 3: Read-Path Cleanup + UI Guards

### Step 13: Disable per-clip resize handles on group members (`src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.tsx`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Edit** `ClipAction.tsx` (`src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.tsx:23, 162–240`): when `isInPinnedShotGroup`, render no per-clip resize handles. The group's left/right handles live on the overlay (Step 8).

### Step 14: Selection model unchanged (`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineSelection.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Decision (settled):** Single click selects the inner clip. The asset-state-only context menu is already in place and the broader edit surface (Step 11) now reroutes clip-level mutations on group members to group-level operations, so there is no path by which a clip-level selection can break a group.
2. **Verify** `useTimelineSelection`/`useMultiSelect` hold IDs only, not stale positions.

### Step 15: Delete dead cohesion code (`src/tools/video-editor/lib/resolve-overlaps.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.tsx`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipResize.ts`, `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useShotGroups.ts`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Delete** (only after callers migrate): `getShotGroupAdjacent`, the in-array-order contiguity check, `deferredPinnedGroupClipIds` and `expandPinnedGroupDrag`, the collective-extent calc in `resolveGroupOverlaps`, and the interior auto-shift transaction in `useClipResize` for the group-member case (non-group clips keep auto-shift). Per CLAUDE.md: every change should make the codebase smaller or more explicit.
2. **Audit** `useShotGroups.ts:26–55` — the "no live clipIds" filter can now trust `children`.

---

## Phase 4: Validation

### Step 16: Update existing tests + add new ones
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Update** existing tests for the new shape (`start`+`children`):
   - `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useShotGroups.test.ts`
   - `src/tools/video-editor/lib/serialize.test.ts` — add a legacy-config migration round-trip case
   - `src/tools/video-editor/lib/multi-drag-utils.test.ts` — assert group-entity moves call `moveGroup`
   - `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useClipDrag.test.tsx` — assert single `GroupDragEntry` recorded; deferred-expansion gone
   - `src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/ClipAction.test.tsx` — no per-clip resize handles for group members
   - `src/tools/video-editor/components/TimelineEditor/TimelineCanvas.test.tsx` — `positionedShotGroups` reads from `group.start`/`children` (leave the pre-existing failing `data-shot-group-kind="pinned"` case alone per the brief)
   - `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/__tests__/resolve-overlaps.test.ts` — caller-provided extent API
   - **`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useExternalDrop.test.tsx`** — assert dropped shots create groups with `start` + spatially-ordered `children`
   - **`src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useTimelineTrackManagement.test.ts`** — assert ArrowUp/ArrowDown / `moveSelectedClipsToTrack` on a group member moves the whole group, not just the one clip
   - Any `useClipEditing` / `useKeyboardShortcuts` tests covering split/delete on selected clips — update for the new redirect-on-group-member rules
2. **Add** `src/tools/video-editor/lib/pinned-group-cohesion.test.ts`:
   - Property test: random sequences of `moveGroup` / `resizeGroupEdge` / `replaceGroupChildren` always yield contiguous, spatially-sorted children after projection.
   - Cross-track group move via the group entity.
   - Negative-`start` clamp.
3. **Add** a small integration test that exercises the commit boundary: build a `TimelineConfig` with a legacy group, run it through `buildDataFromCurrentRegistry`, assert (a) member clip starts in the resulting `resolvedConfig.clips` match `group.start + child.offset`, (b) `signature` is computed from the projected config (not the input), (c) a follow-up `applyEdit({ type: 'config', ... })` with a pre-projection config still ends up projected.

### Step 17: Type check + full suite
**Scope:** Small

1. `npx tsc --noEmit`
2. `vitest run src/tools/video-editor/`

### Step 18: Manual smoke (info)
**Scope:** Small

1. Pin → drag (incl. t=0) → drag cross-track → resize outer edges → attempt interior resize (blocked) → keyboard ArrowUp/ArrowDown on a selected group member (whole group moves) → keyboard delete/split on a group member (delete-shot semantics / split blocked) → drop a shot from the panel onto the timeline (group has `start`+`children`) → trigger sync mid-drag (sync deferred until release) → shot label menu actions → Remotion preview unchanged.

---

## Execution Order
1. Phase 1 (model + projection + clone helpers + migration + tests). Land before any UI touches; the projection wiring in Step 3 is the load-bearing change.
2. Phase 2 in order: Step 7 (drag) → Step 8 (resize) → Step 9 (sync + coordinator guard) → Step 10 (external drop) → Step 11 (edit surface) → Step 12 (shot-group menu verify).
3. Phase 3 (UI guards + dead code) after Phase 2 compiles.
4. Phase 4 tests run continuously; full suite + tsc last.

## Validation Order
1. `pinned-group-projection.test.ts` (Step 6) — fastest, validates the core invariant.
2. `pinned-group-cohesion.test.ts` (Step 16) — property test for the whole write path.
3. Updated existing tests in `src/tools/video-editor/`, including the newly-added `useExternalDrop` and `useTimelineTrackManagement` updates.
4. Commit-boundary integration test (Step 16.3).
5. `npx tsc --noEmit`.
6. Full `vitest run src/tools/video-editor/`.
7. Manual smoke (Step 18, info-level).

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-041",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the actual export surface in `supabase/functions/ai-timeline-agent/llm/messages.ts` and its consumers. step 6 says to 'remove local `isrecord`' from `messages.ts` and import it from `utils.ts`, but without also preserving `messages.ts` as an exporter or rewiring `index.ts`, `db.ts`, `tool-calls.ts`, and `selectedclips.ts`, that change would break the module graph immediately.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-055",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the render-gating cleanup plan against remotion's runtime exports in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/cancel-render.js:46-70`. `cancelrender()` stores an error and throws to abort the render; it is not a benign cleanup for an abandoned `delayrender` handle, so step 2's instruction to call `cancelrender(handle)` on unmount is technically wrong and would abort preview or export with an error.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-056",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked how remotion itself acquires delay handles in `node_modules/remotion/dist/cjs/iframe.js:8-18`. remotion's own components create the handle synchronously with `usestate(() => delayrender(...))`; the plan says 'calls delayrender() on mount', which reads like an effect-time action and leaves the initial-frame blocking behavior underspecified compared with the actual library pattern.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-057",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new `useaudioparam()` sketch against `src/tools/video-editor/effects/compileeffect.tsx:1-2`. the plan defines it in terms of `interpolate(audio[binding.source], ...)` but does not explicitly import `interpolate` into the new hook file or simplify it to straight arithmetic, so the helper's implementation contract is still looser than it needs to be.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-audio-reactive-effects-to-20260406-0440"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-070",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked revised step 2.4 against `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useclipresize.ts:76-100`. the plan still says to clamp audio by writing back to `action.end`, but the current hook treats the callback `action` as input data and then creates fresh row actions from it. mutating that caller-owned object is unnecessary and risks coupling the resize hook to outside references instead of using a local `clampedend` variable.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-video-and-audio-clip-20260406-0616"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-076",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the no-duration-fetch decision against `src/tools/travel-between-images/hooks/video/useshotfinalvideos.ts:19-37`, `src/tools/video-editor/lib/config-utils.ts:17-35`, and `src/tools/video-editor/compositions/visualclip.tsx:114-123`. the repository still derives clip length from `to - from`, and the final-video query still provides no duration metadata, so the revised plan still cannot prove that `to: endtime - starttime` is safe for shorter source media; a short final video can still be asked to play longer than its actual content.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-a-final-video-available-20260406-0801"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-077",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hand-built replacement clip against the existing insert path in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/useassetmanagement.ts:201-252,262-271` and serialization in `src/tools/video-editor/hooks/usetimelinecommit.ts:210-216`. the revised `timelineeditor` sketch still bypasses current insertion invariants such as manual-track geometry defaults and explicit `cliporder` updates, so replacement clips on manual visual tracks will not be initialized the same way as other inserted video clips and may serialize in a different per-track order.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "add-a-final-video-available-20260406-0801"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-089",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the new `pinnedshotgroupsoverride` design is still underspecified against `materializedata()`. `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so if the executor literally follows step 2.4 and overwrites `pinnedshotgroups` on an already-materialized `timelinedata` before `commitdata()`, the saved config and the cached `stablesignature` will diverge unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-090",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the revised grouped-drag plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still leaves `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` stale on cross-track moves. the data model stores `trackid`, and current drag/move helpers only update rows/meta/clip order, so a pinned group dragged to another track would keep pointing at the old track unless the drag commit also patches group metadata.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-105",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked step 2.4 against `materializedata()` in `usetimelinecommit` and `assembletimelinedata()` in `timeline-data.ts`. the plan still says to overwrite `pinnedshotgroups` after `materializedata()` returns, but `assembletimelinedata()` computes `stablesignature` from the config it receives, so mutating the config afterward would leave signature-derived state stale unless the data is rebuilt from the updated config.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the grouped-drag plan against `useclipdrag` and `usetimelinetrackmanagement.movecliptorow()`. the plan fixes the pointer-down selection regression, but it still does not say how `pinnedshotgroup.trackid` is updated when a grouped drag or move lands on a different row, so the overlay and menu state can still point at the old track after a successful cross-track move.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the cross-track multi-drag new-track branch in `useclipdrag`. that path still commits through `applyedit({ type: 'config', resolvedconfig })` using `buildconfigfromdragresult()`, and the revised plan does not explain how pinned-group metadata is updated atomically on that path, so grouped drags that create a new track are still under-specified even beyond the stale-`trackid` problem.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    }

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-038",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the current callers of `isrecord` from `llm/messages.ts`. `index.ts` uses it to validate `rawsession`, `db.ts` uses it on db rows, `tool-calls.ts` uses it on parsed tool-call payloads and nested `function` objects, and `selectedclips.ts` uses it while normalizing attachments, so preserving that import contract matters to four different call paths.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "cleanup-and-quality-pass-on-20260404-0138"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-095",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the current `commitdata()` and history pipeline treat each `applyedit()` as its own commit boundary. because step 16 proposes a post-commit reconcile edit after deletions, the actual callers here matter: without `skiphistory` or transaction coalescing, one user action will produce two history events and likely two saves.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-096",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: `handleassetdrop()` and the external-drop wrapper are not pure builders; they immediately mutate editor state and call `applyedit()` per asset. that means the plan cannot literally reuse those callers for atomic shot-drop creation without first extracting a lower-level helper, or it will violate its own \"single applyedit\" requirement.",
      "occurrence_count": 2,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-111",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the actual commit/history callers around step 18. `commitdata()` emits `beforecommit` and schedules save on every edit, and `usetimelinehistory.onbeforecommit()` pushes undo entries per commit boundary, so a reconcile-after-delete `applyedit` still fragments one user action into multiple history/save operations unless the plan also changes those caller contracts.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-115",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: checked the multi-drag callers in `useclipdrag`. the 'new track' grouped-drag branch still routes through `buildconfigfromdragresult()` and `applyedit({ type: 'config' })`, so the revised plan still does not fully handle every caller shape for grouped moves once pinned-group metadata becomes part of the timeline state.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "implement-two-tier-shot-20260406-1054"
      ]
    }
  ],
